Clear enough that it feels like he’s right behind me, even though I know he’s… gone.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, do you believe he is actually there in that moment?\n\nPatient: In the moment… yes. Or it feels that way. Part of me knows he’s dead, but when I hear him behind me, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Did Garrett die recently?\n\nPatient: About a month ago. That’s when Garrett died, and… things have been bad since then.\n\nDoctor: Aside from hearing Garrett’s voice, have you had any other experiences that felt strange or unreal, like seeing things other people couldn’t see or feeling that someone was trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: No… not really like that. I mean, the thing that feels unreal is Garrett’s voice. I hear him like he’s right behind me—on the trail, or in my painting room—and for that moment it feels completely real, even though I know he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Since Garrett died, have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve felt… low, I guess, but I haven’t really been tracking it that way.\n\nDoctor: Since Garrett died, have you been feeling guilty or blaming yourself for his death?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. I keep going over the last things I said to Garrett, what I should’ve done differently. And I keep thinking about patients I cancelled on, like I failed them too.\n\nIt just gets to where I feel like… maybe everyone would be better off if I wasn’t in the way.\n\nDoctor: Have you wanted to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have.\n\nNot all the time as some big plan, but I keep thinking I don’t want to wake up. And there have been moments where it felt… too close. I told my wife I shouldn’t be alone because I didn’t trust myself.\n\nDoctor: Since Garrett died, have you felt down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say nearly every day. It’s been heavy, and I’ve been low, but I haven’t really been keeping track like that.\n\nDoctor: Since Garrett died, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean… the things I usually use to stay grounded don’t really do that now.\n\nPainting, guitar, rehearsals… even work with patients. It all just feels flat. Like I’m doing the motions, but I’m not really… in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you made a specific plan to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Not… not a specific plan, no.\n\nIt’s more that I keep thinking I don’t want to wake up. And sometimes it feels close enough that I told my wife I shouldn’t be alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your thoughts or speech getting so jumbled that other people have trouble following you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes.\n\nIt’s less like I can’t talk at all, more like my mind gets ahead of me. I’ll start thinking about one patient note, and then suddenly it’s five other things, some project, a hike I took, then the funeral… and I’m trying to grab onto one thread before it’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Outside the moment when you hear Garrett’s voice, do you believe Garrett is actually still alive?\n\nPatient: No… not when I’m clear-headed.\n\nI know he’s dead. It’s just… when I hear his voice, it feels like he’s right there. Like behind me or in the room. In that moment it’s hard to hold onto what I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent illness, head injury, or seizure?\n\nPatient: No… not that I can think of. I haven’t had a seizure or hit my head recently. No recent illness that stands out.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, is he usually saying things that fit with your guilt or that you should die?\n\nPatient: It… it usually feels like him. Garrett.\n\nNot always a clear sentence, but when it happens it’s as clear as if he’s right behind me—on the trail, or in my painting room. In that moment it feels completely real, even if some part of me knows he’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear Garrett’s voice only when you’re falling asleep or just waking up?\n\nPatient: No… it’s not just then.\n\nIt’s happened when I’m awake—on the trail, or in my painting room. It sounds like Garrett’s right behind me. I know he’s dead, but in that moment it… it feels real.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, does it bring you back into the moment of his death like you’re reliving it?\n\nPatient: Not exactly… it’s more that I hear him.
